<issue_description>
Solution[<variable>] doesn't work when some parameters are inputs
**Describe the bug**
The inputs are not making their way to all the things that need to be evaluated somehow. The example below will work if current is the only input but fails when electrode height is one. Something to do with mesh

**To Reproduce**
```
import pybamm
import numpy as np

pybamm.set_logging_level('DEBUG')

I_typical = 1.0

def current_function(t):
    return pybamm.InputParameter("Current")

model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
geometry = model.default_geometry
param = model.default_parameter_values
param.update(
    {
        "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
        "Current function [A]": current_function,
        "Current": "[input]",
        "Electrode height [m]": "[input]",
    }
)
param.process_model(model)
param.process_geometry(geometry)
var = pybamm.standard_spatial_vars
var_pts = {var.x_n: 5, var.x_s: 5, var.x_p: 5, var.r_n: 10, var.r_p: 10}
spatial_methods = model.default_spatial_methods

solver = model.default_solver
sim = pybamm.Simulation(
    model=model,
    geometry=geometry,
    parameter_values=param,
    var_pts=var_pts,
    spatial_methods=spatial_methods,
    solver=solver,
)

inputs = {
        'Current': I_typical,
        'Electrode height [m]': 0.1
        }

sim.solve(t_eval=np.linspace(0, 0.01, 100), inputs=inputs)
print(sim.solution['Time [h]'](sim.solution.t))
```


</issue_description>

Key technical details:
- Uses pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM() model
- Parameters updated with both "Current" and "Electrode height [m]" as "[input]"
- Uses pybamm.InputParameter for current function
- Process model and geometry with these inputs
- Simulation.solve() called with inputs dictionary
- Trying to access sim.solution['Time [h]'] after solving

The issue suggests that inputs aren't making their way to all components that need evaluation, particularly when dealing with mesh-related parameters like electrode height.

Steps to reproduce:
1. Set up SPM model with typical parameters
2. Update parameters to make both Current and Electrode height as inputs
3. Process model and geometry 
4. Create simulation
5. Solve with inputs including both Current and Electrode height
6. Try to access solution variable - this should fail

Best practices for fixing:
- Need to ensure inputs propagate correctly through all evaluation paths
- Pay special attention to mesh generation and spatial discretization
- Make sure parameter processing handles input parameters properly in all contexts
- Minimal changes to preserve existing functionality</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me first explore the repository structure to understand the codebase.<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the files and directories up to 2 levels deep in /workspace, excluding hidden items:

     2	
     3	[![travis](https://travis-ci.org/pybamm-team/PyBaMM.svg?branch=master)](https://travis-ci.org/pybamm-team/PyBaMM)
     4	[![Build status](https://ci.appveyor.com/api/projects/status/xdje8jnhuj0ye1jc/branch/master?svg=true)](https://ci.appveyor.com/project/martinjrobins/pybamm/branch/master)

     9	
    10	Python Battery Mathematical Modelling solves continuum models for batteries, using both numerical methods and asymptotic analysis.
    11	
    12	## How do I use PyBaMM?
    13	
    14	The easiest way to use PyBaMM is to run a 1C constant-current discharge with a model of your choice with all the default settings:
    15	```python3
    16	import pybamm
    17	model = pybamm.lithium_ion.DFN() # Doyle-Fuller-Newman model
    18	sim = pybamm.Simulation(model)
    19	sim.solve()
    20	sim.plot()
    21	```
    22	However, much greater customisation is available. It is possible to change the physics, parameter values, geometry, submesh type,  number of submesh points, methods for spatial discretisation and solver for integration (see DFN [script](examples/scripts/DFN.py) or [notebook](examples/notebooks/models/dfn.ipynb)).
    23	
    24	For new users we recommend the [Getting Started](examples/notebooks/Getting%20Started/) guides. These are intended to be very simple step-by-step guides to show the basic functionality of PyBaMM.
    25	
    26	Further details can be found in a number of [detailed examples](examples/notebooks/README.md), hosted here on
    27	github. In addition, there is a [full API documentation](http://pybamm.readthedocs.io/),
    28	hosted on [Read The Docs](readthedocs.io). A set of slides giving an overview of PyBaMM
    29	can be found
    30	[here](https://github.com/pybamm-team/pybamm_summary_slides/blob/master/pybamm.pdf).
    31	
    32	For further examples, see the list of repositories that use PyBaMM [here](https://github.com/pybamm-team/pybamm-example-results)
    33	
    34	## How can I obtain & install PyBaMM?
    35	
    36	### Linux
    37	
    38	For instructions on installing PyBaMM on Debian-based distributions, please see [here](INSTALL-LINUX.md)
    39	
    40	### Windows
    41	
    42	We recommend using Windows Subsystem for Linux to install PyBaMM on a Windows OS, for

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python
     2	#
     3	# Runs all unit tests included in PyBaMM.
     4	#
     5	# The code in this file is adapted from Pints
     6	# (see https://github.com/pints-team/pints)
     7	#
     8	import re

    17	def run_code_tests(executable=False, folder: str = "unit"):
    18	    """
    19	    Runs tests, exits if they don't finish.
    20	    Parameters
    21	    ----------
    22	    executable : bool (default False)
    23	        If True, tests are run in subprocesses using the executable 'python'.
    24	        Must be True for travis tests (otherwise tests always 'pass')
    25	    folder : str
    26	        Which folder to run the tests from (unit, integration or both ('all'))
    27	    """
    28	    if folder == "all":
    29	        tests = "tests/"
    30	    else:
    31	        tests = "tests/" + folder
    32	        if folder == "unit":
    33	            pybamm.settings.debug_mode = True
    34	    if executable is False:
    35	        suite = unittest.defaultTestLoader.discover(tests, pattern="test*.py")
    36	        unittest.TextTestRunner(verbosity=2).run(suite)
    37	    else:
    38	        print("Running {} tests with executable 'python'".format(folder))
    39	        cmd = ["python", "-m", "unittest", "discover", "-v", tests]
    40	        p = subprocess.Popen(cmd)
    41	        try:
    42	            ret = p.wait()
    43	        except KeyboardInterrupt:
    44	            try:
    45	                p.terminate()
    46	            except OSError:
    47	                pass
    48	            p.wait()
    49	            print("")
    50	            sys.exit(1)
    51	        if ret != 0:
    52	            sys.exit(ret)
    53

    55	def run_flake8():
    56	    """
    57	    Runs flake8 in a subprocess, exits if it doesn't finish.
    58	    """
    59	    print("Running flake8 ... ")
    60	    sys.stdout.flush()
    61	    p = subprocess.Popen(["flake8"], stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
    62	    try:
    63	        ret = p.wait()
    64	    except KeyboardInterrupt:
    65	        try:
    66	            p.terminate()
    67	        except OSError:
    68	            pass
    69	        p.wait()
    70	        print("")
    71	        sys.exit(1)
    72	    if ret == 0:
    73	        print("ok")
    74	    else:
    75	        print("FAILED")
    76	        sys.exit(ret)

   103	def run_notebook_and_scripts(skip_slow_books=False, executable="python"):
   104	    """
   105	    Runs Jupyter notebook tests. Exits if they fail.
   106	    """
   107	    # Ignore slow books?
   108	    ignore_list = []
   109	    if skip_slow_books and os.path.isfile(".slow-books"):
   110	        with open(".slow-books", "r") as f:
   111	            for line in f.readlines():
   112	                line = line.strip()
   113	                if not line or line[:1] == "#":
   114	                    continue
   115	                if not line.startswith("examples/"):
   116	                    line = "examples/" + line
   117	                if not line.endswith(".ipynb"):
   118	                    line = line + ".ipynb"
   119	                if not os.path.isfile(line):
   120	                    raise Exception("Slow notebook note found: " + line)
   121	                ignore_list.append(line)
   122	
   123	    # Scan and run
   124	    print("Testing notebooks and scripts with executable `" + str(executable) + "`")
   125	    if not scan_for_nb_and_scripts("examples", True, executable, ignore_list):
   126	        print("\nErrors encountered in notebooks")
   127	        sys.exit(1)
   128	    print("\nOK")
   129	
   130	
   131	def scan_for_nb_and_scripts(root, recursive=True, executable="python", ignore_list=[]):
   132	    """
   133	    Scans for, and tests, all notebooks and scripts in a directory.
   134	    """
   135	    ok = True
   136	    debug = False
   137	
   138	    # Scan path
   139	    for filename in os.listdir(root):
   140	        path = os.path.join(root, filename)
   141	        if path in ignore_list:
   142	            print("Skipping slow book: " + path)
   143	            continue
   144	
   145	        # Recurse into subdirectories
   146	        if recursive and os.path.isdir(path):
   147	            # Ignore hidden directories
   148	            if filename[:1] == ".":
   149	                continue
   150	            ok &= scan_for_nb_and_scripts(path, recursive, executable)
   151	
   152	        # Test notebooks
   153	        if os.path.splitext(path)[1] == ".ipynb":
   154	            if debug:
   155	                print(path)
   156	            else:
   157	                ok &= test_notebook(path, executable)
   158	        # Test scripts
   159	        elif os.path.splitext(path)[1] == ".py":
   160	            if debug:
   161	                print(path)
   162	            else:
   163	                ok &= test_script(path, executable)
   164	
   165	    # Return True if every notebook is ok
   166	    return ok
   167	
   168	
   169	def test_notebook(path, executable="python"):
   170	    """
   171	    Tests a single notebook, exists if it doesn't finish.
   172	    """
   173	    import nbconvert
   174	    import pybamm
   175	
   176	    b = pybamm.Timer()
   177	    print("Test " + path + " ... ", end="")
   178	    sys.stdout.flush()
   179	
   180	    # Load notebook, convert to python
   181	    e = nbconvert.exporters.PythonExporter()
   182	    code, __ = e.from_filename(path)
   183	
   184	    # Remove coding statement, if present
   185	    code = "\n".join([x for x in code.splitlines() if x[:9] != "# coding"])
   186	
   187	    # Tell matplotlib not to produce any figures
   188	    env = dict(os.environ)
   189	    env["MPLBACKEND"] = "Template"
   190	
   191	    # Run in subprocess
   192	    cmd = [executable] + ["-c", code]
   193	    try:
   194	        p = subprocess.Popen(
   195	            cmd, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, env=env
   196	        )
   197	        stdout, stderr = p.communicate()
   198	        # TODO: Use p.communicate(timeout=3600) if Python3 only
   199	        if p.returncode != 0:
   200	            # Show failing code, output and errors before returning
   201	            print("ERROR")
   202	            print("-- script " + "-" * (79 - 10))
   203	            for i, line in enumerate(code.splitlines()):
   204	                j = str(1 + i)
   205	                print(j + " " * (5 - len(j)) + line)
   206	            print("-- stdout " + "-" * (79 - 10))
   207	            print(str(stdout, "utf-8"))
   208	            print("-- stderr " + "-" * (79 - 10))
   209	            print(str(stderr, "utf-8"))
   210	            print("-" * 79)
   211	            return False
   212	    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   213	        p.terminate()
   214	        print("ABORTED")
   215	        sys.exit(1)
   216	
   217	    # Sucessfully run
   218	    print("ok (" + b.format() + ")")
   219	    return True
   220	
   221

   223	    """
   224	    Tests a single notebook, exists if it doesn't finish.
   225	    """

   262	
   263	def export_notebook(ipath, opath):
   264	    """
   265	    Exports the notebook at `ipath` to a python file at `opath`.
   266	    """
   267	    import nbconvert
   268	    from traitlets.config import Config
   269	
   270	    # Create nbconvert configuration to ignore text cells
   271	    c = Config()
   272	    c.TemplateExporter.exclude_markdown = True
   273	
   274	    # Load notebook, convert to python
   275	    e = nbconvert.exporters.PythonExporter(config=c)
   276	    code, __ = e.from_filename(ipath)
   277	
   278	    # Remove "In [1]:" comments
   279	    r = re.compile(r"(\s*)# In\[([^]]*)\]:(\s)*")
   280	    code = r.sub("\n\n", code)
   281	
   282	    # Store as executable script file
   283	    with open(opath, "w") as f:
   284	        f.write("#!/usr/bin/env python")
   285	        f.write(code)
   286	    os.chmod(opath, 0o775)
   287	
   288	
   289	if __name__ == "__main__":
   290	    # Set up argument parsing
   291	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
   292	        description="Run unit tests for PyBaMM.",
   293	        epilog="To run individual unit tests, use e.g. '$ tests/unit/test_timer.py'",
   294	    )
   295	    # Unit tests
   296	    parser.add_argument(
   297	        "--unit",
   298	        action="store_true",
   299	        help="Run unit tests using the `python` interpreter.",
   300	    )
   301	    parser.add_argument(
   302	        "--nosub",
   303	        action="store_true",
   304	        help="Run unit tests without starting a subprocess.",
   305	    )
   306	    # Daily tests vs unit tests
   307	    parser.add_argument(
   308	        "--folder",
   309	        nargs=1,
   310	        default=["unit"],
   311	        choices=["unit", "integration", "all"],
   312	        help="Which folder to run the tests from.",
   313	    )
   314	    # Notebook tests
   315	    parser.add_argument(
   316	        "--examples",
   317	        action="store_true",
   318	        help="Test only the fast Jupyter notebooks and scripts in `examples`.",
   319	    )
   320	    parser.add_argument(
   321	        "--allexamples",
   322	        action="store_true",
   323	        help="Test all Jupyter notebooks and scripts in `examples`.",
   324	    )
   325	    parser.add_argument(
   326	        "-debook",
   327	        nargs=2,
   328	        metavar=("in", "out"),
   329	        help="Export a Jupyter notebook to a Python file for manual testing.",

import numpy as np

pybamm.set_logging_level('DEBUG')

I_typical = 1.0

def current_function(t):
    return pybamm.InputParameter("Current")

model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
geometry = model.default_geometry
param = model.default_parameter_values
param.update(
    {
        "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
        "Current function [A]": current_function,
        "Current": "[input]",
        # "Electrode height [m]": "[input]",  # Comment this out to test with single input
    }
)
param.process_model(model)
param.process_geometry(geometry)
var = pybamm.standard_spatial_vars
var_pts = {var.x_n: 5, var.x_s: 5, var.x_p: 5, var.r_n: 10, var.r_p: 10}
spatial_methods = model.default_spatial_methods

solver = model.default_solver
sim = pybamm.Simulation(
    model=model,
    geometry=geometry,
    parameter_values=param,
    var_pts=var_pts,
    spatial_methods=spatial_methods,
    solver=solver,
)

inputs = {
        'Current': I_typical,
        # 'Electrode height [m]': 0.1
        }

try:
    sim.solve(t_eval=np.linspace(0, 0.01, 100), inputs=inputs)
    print("Simulation solved successfully")
    print(sim.solution['Time [h]'](sim.solution.t))
except Exception as e:
    print(f"Error occurred: {e}")

The problem is that when we try to access variables from the solution using bracket notation `solution[<variable>]`, it fails because the solution object doesn't have those variables precomputed. The solution only contains `Time [h]` initially, and other variables need to be computed on demand.

But looking at the original issue description, it says it should work with one input but not with two inputs. Let me test that hypothesis:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pybamm-team__PyBaMM__0.1/test_compare_inputs.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>import pybamm

pybamm.set_logging_level('INFO')  # Reduce verbosity

I_typical = 1.0

def current_function(t):

geometry1 = model1.default_geometry

    {
        "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
        "Current function [A]": current_function,
        "Current": "[input]",

    }
)

)

        }

     1	#
     2	# Native PyBaMM Meshes
     3	#
     4	import numpy as np
     5	import pybamm
     6	
     7	
     8	class Mesh(dict):
     9	    """
    10	    Mesh contains a list of submeshes on each subdomain.
    11	
    12	    **Extends**: dict
    13	
    14	    Parameters
    15	    ----------
    16	
    17	    geometry : :class: `Geometry`
    18	        contains the geometry of the problem.
    19	    submesh_types: dict
    20	        contains the types of submeshes to use (e.g. Uniform1DSubMesh)
    21	    submesh_pts: dict
    22	        contains the number of points on each subdomain
    23	
    24	    """
    25	
    26	    def __init__(self, geometry, submesh_types, var_pts):
    27	        super().__init__()
    28	        # convert var_pts to an id dict
    29	        var_id_pts = {var.id: pts for var, pts in var_pts.items()}
    30	
    31	        # create submesh_pts from var_pts
    32	        submesh_pts = {}
    33	        for domain in geometry:
    34	            # create mesh generator if just class is passed (will throw an error
    35	            # later if the mesh needed parameters)
    36	            if not isinstance(
    37	                submesh_types[domain], pybamm.MeshGenerator
    38	            ) and issubclass(submesh_types[domain], pybamm.SubMesh):
    39	                submesh_types[domain] = pybamm.MeshGenerator(submesh_types[domain])
    40	            # Zero dimensional submesh case (only one point)
    41	            if issubclass(submesh_types[domain].submesh_type, pybamm.SubMesh0D):
    42	                submesh_pts[domain] = 1
    43	            # other cases
    44	            else:
    45	                submesh_pts[domain] = {}
    46	                if len(list(geometry[domain]["primary"].keys())) > 2:
    47	                    raise pybamm.GeometryError
    48	                for prim_sec in list(geometry[domain].keys()):
    49	                    # skip over tabs key
    50	                    if prim_sec != "tabs":
    51	                        for var in list(geometry[domain][prim_sec].keys()):
    52	                            # Raise error if the number of points for a particular
    53	                            # variable haven't been provided, unless that variable
    54	                            # doesn't appear in the geometry
    55	                            if (
    56	                                var.id not in var_id_pts.keys()
    57	                                and var.domain[0] in geometry.keys()
    58	                            ):
    59	                                raise KeyError(
    60	                                    """
    61	                                    Points not given for a variable in domain {}
    62	                                    """.format(
    63	                                        domain
    64	                                    )
    65	                                )
    66	                            # Otherwise add to the dictionary of submesh points
    67	                            submesh_pts[domain][var.id] = var_id_pts[var.id]
    68	        self.submesh_pts = submesh_pts
    69	
    70	        # Input domain order manually
    71	        self.domain_order = []
    72	        # First the macroscale domains, whose order we care about
    73	        for domain in ["negative electrode", "separator", "positive electrode"]:
    74	            if domain in geometry:
    75	                self.domain_order.append(domain)
    76	        # Then the remaining domains
    77	        for domain in geometry:
    78	            if domain not in ["negative electrode", "separator", "positive electrode"]:
    79	                self.domain_order.append(domain)
    80	
    81	        # evaluate any expressions in geometry
    82	        for domain in geometry:
    83	            for prim_sec_tabs, variables in geometry[domain].items():
    84	                # process tab information if using 1 or 2D current collectors
    85	                if prim_sec_tabs == "tabs":
    86	                    for tab, position_size in variables.items():
    87	                        for position_size, sym in position_size.items():
    88	                            if isinstance(sym, pybamm.Symbol):
    89	                                sym_eval = sym.evaluate()
    90	                                geometry[domain][prim_sec_tabs][tab][
    91	                                    position_size
    92	                                ] = sym_eval
    93	                else:
    94	                    for spatial_variable, spatial_limits in variables.items():
    95	                        for lim, sym in spatial_limits.items():
    96	                            if isinstance(sym, pybamm.Symbol):
    97	                                sym_eval = sym.evaluate()
    98	                                geometry[domain][prim_sec_tabs][spatial_variable][
    99	                                    lim
   100	                                ] = sym_eval
   101	
   102	        # Create submeshes
   103	        for domain in geometry:
   104	            # repeat mesh if domain has secondary dimension
   105	            if "secondary" in geometry[domain].keys():
   106	                repeats = 1
   107	                for var in geometry[domain]["secondary"].keys():
   108	                    repeats *= submesh_pts[domain][var.id]  # note (specific to FV)
   109	            else:
   110	                repeats = 1
   111	            # create submesh, passing tab information if provided
   112	            if "tabs" in geometry[domain].keys():
   113	                self[domain] = [
   114	                    submesh_types[domain](
   115	                        geometry[domain]["primary"],
   116	                        submesh_pts[domain],
   117	                        geometry[domain]["tabs"],
   118	                    )
   119	                ] * repeats
   120	            else:
   121	                self[domain] = [
   122	                    submesh_types[domain](
   123	                        geometry[domain]["primary"], submesh_pts[domain]
   124	                    )
   125	                ] * repeats
   126	
   127	        # add ghost meshes
   128	        self.add_ghost_meshes()
   129	
   130	    def combine_submeshes(self, *submeshnames):
   131	        """Combine submeshes into a new submesh, using self.submeshclass
   132	        Raises pybamm.DomainError if submeshes to be combined do not match up (edges are
   133	        not aligned).
   134	
   135	        Parameters
   136	        ----------
   137	        submeshnames: list of str
   138	            The names of the submeshes to be combined
   139	
   140	        Returns
   141	        -------
   142	        submesh: :class:`self.submeshclass`
   143	            A new submesh with the class defined by self.submeshclass
   144	        """
   145	        # Check that the final edge of each submesh is the same as the first edge of the
   146	        # next submesh
   147	        for i in range(len(submeshnames) - 1):
   148	            for j in range(len(self[submeshnames[i]])):
   149	                if (
   150	                    self[submeshnames[i]][j].edges[-1]
   151	                    != self[submeshnames[i + 1]][j].edges[0]
   152	                ):
   153	                    raise pybamm.DomainError("submesh edges are not aligned")
   154	
   155	            coord_sys = self[submeshnames[i]][0].coord_sys
   156	            coord_sys_r = self[submeshnames[i + 1]][0].coord_sys
   157	            if coord_sys != coord_sys_r:
   158	                raise pybamm.DomainError(
   159	                    "trying to combine two meshes in different coordinate systems"
   160	                )
   161	        submeshes = [None] * len(self[submeshnames[0]])
   162	        # Hack for the special case of current collector
   163	        if submeshnames == ("current collector",) and isinstance(
   164	            self[submeshnames[0]][0].edges, dict
   165	        ):
   166	            return self[submeshnames[0]]
   167	        for i in range(len(self[submeshnames[0]])):
   168	            combined_submesh_edges = np.concatenate(
   169	                [self[submeshnames[0]][i].edges]
   170	                + [self[submeshname][i].edges[1:] for submeshname in submeshnames[1:]]
   171	            )
   172	            coord_sys = self[submeshnames[0]][i].coord_sys
   173	            submeshes[i] = pybamm.SubMesh1D(combined_submesh_edges, coord_sys)
   174	        return submeshes
   175	
   176	    def add_ghost_meshes(self):
   177	        """
   178	        Create meshes for potential ghost nodes on either side of each submesh, using
   179	        self.submeshclass
   180	        This will be useful for calculating the gradient with Dirichlet BCs.
   181	        """
   182	        # Get all submeshes relating to space (i.e. exclude time)
   183	        submeshes = [
   184	            (domain, submesh_list)
   185	            for domain, submesh_list in self.items()
   186	            if not isinstance(
   187	                submesh_list[0], (pybamm.SubMesh0D, pybamm.ScikitSubMesh2D)
   188	            )
   189	        ]
   190	        for domain, submesh_list in submeshes:
   191	
   192	            self[domain + "_left ghost cell"] = [None] * len(submesh_list)
   193	            self[domain + "_right ghost cell"] = [None] * len(submesh_list)
   194	            for i, submesh in enumerate(submesh_list):
   195	                edges = submesh.edges
   196	
   197	                # left ghost cell: two edges, one node, to the left of existing submesh
   198	                lgs_edges = np.array([2 * edges[0] - edges[1], edges[0]])
   199	                self[domain + "_left ghost cell"][i] = pybamm.SubMesh1D(
   200	                    lgs_edges, submesh.coord_sys
   201	                )
   202	
   203	                # right ghost cell: two edges, one node, to the right of
   204	                # existing submesh
   205	                rgs_edges = np.array([edges[-1], 2 * edges[-1] - edges[-2]])
   206	                self[domain + "_right ghost cell"][i] = pybamm.SubMesh1D(
   207	                    rgs_edges, submesh.coord_sys
   208	                )
   209	
   210	
   211	class SubMesh:
   212	    """
   213	    Base submesh class.
   214	    Contains the position of the nodes, the number of mesh points, and
   215	    (optionally) information about the tab locations.
   216	    """
   217	
   218	    def __init__(self):
   219	        pass
   220

   222	class MeshGenerator:
   223	    """
   224	    Base class for mesh generator objects that are used to generate submeshes.
   225	
   226	    Parameters
   227	    ----------
   228	
   229	    submesh_type: :class:`pybamm.SubMesh`
   230	        The type of submesh to use (e.g. Uniform1DSubMesh).
   231	    submesh_params: dict, optional
   232	        Contains any parameters required by the submesh.
   233	    """
   234	
   235	    def __init__(self, submesh_type, submesh_params=None):
   236	        self.submesh_type = submesh_type
   237	        self.submesh_params = submesh_params or {}
   238	
   239	    def __call__(self, lims, npts, tabs=None):
   240	        return self.submesh_type(lims, npts, tabs, **self.submesh_params)
   241	
   242	    def __repr__(self):
   243	        return "Generator for {}".format(self.submesh_type.__name__)
   244

    10	class ParameterValues(dict):

    12	    The parameter values for a simulation.
    13	
    14	    Parameters
    15	    ----------
    16	    values : dict or string
    17	        Explicit set of parameters, or reference to a file of parameters
    18	        If string, gets passed to read_parameters_csv to read a file.
    19	    chemistry : dict
    20	        Dict of strings for default chemistries. Must be of the form:
    21	        {"base chemistry": base_chemistry,
    22	        "cell": cell_properties_authorYear,
    23	        "anode": anode_chemistry_authorYear,
    24	        "separator": separator_chemistry_authorYear,
    25	        "cathode": cathode_chemistry_authorYear,
    26	        "electrolyte": electrolyte_chemistry_authorYear,
    27	        "experiment": experimental_conditions_authorYear}.
    28	        Then the anode chemistry is loaded from the file
    29	        inputs/parameters/base_chemistry/anodes/anode_chemistry_authorYear, etc.
    30	        Parameters in "cell" should include geometry and current collector properties.
    31	        Parameters in "experiment" should include parameters relating to experimental
    32	        conditions, such as initial conditions and currents.
    33	
    34	    Examples
    35	    --------
    36	    >>> import pybamm
    37	    >>> values = {"some parameter": 1, "another parameter": 2}
    38	    >>> param = pybamm.ParameterValues(values)
    39	    >>> param["some parameter"]
    40	    1
    41	    >>> file = "/input/parameters/lithium-ion/cells/kokam_Marquis2019/parameters.csv"
    42	    >>> param = pybamm.ParameterValues(values=pybamm.root_dir() + file)
    43	    >>> param["Negative current collector thickness [m]"]
    44	    2.5e-05
    45	    >>> param = pybamm.ParameterValues(chemistry=pybamm.parameter_sets.Marquis2019)
    46	    >>> param["Reference temperature [K]"]

    51	    def __init__(self, values=None, chemistry=None):
    52	        # Must provide either values or chemistry, not both (nor neither)
    53	        if values is not None and chemistry is not None:
    54	            raise ValueError(
    55	                """
    56	                Only one of values and chemistry can be provided. To change parameters
    57	                slightly from a chemistry, first load parameters with the chemistry
    58	                (param = pybamm.ParameterValues(chemistry=...)) and then update with
    59	                param.update({dict of values}).
    60	                """
    61	            )
    62	        if values is None and chemistry is None:
    63	            raise ValueError("values and chemistry cannot both be None")
    64	        # First load chemistry
    65	        if chemistry is not None:
    66	            self.update_from_chemistry(chemistry)
    67	        # Then update with values dictionary or file
    68	        if values is not None:
    69	            if isinstance(values, str):
    70	                values = self.read_parameters_csv(values)
    71	            # If base_parameters is a filename, load from that filename
    72	            self.update(values)
    73

    75	        self._processed_symbols = {}

   406	    def process_symbol(self, symbol):
   407	        """Walk through the symbol and replace any Parameter with a Value.
   408	        If a symbol has already been processed, the stored value is returned.
   409	
   410	        Parameters
   411	        ----------
   412	        symbol : :class:`pybamm.Symbol`
   413	            Symbol or Expression tree to set parameters for
   414	
   415	        Returns
   416	        -------
   417	        symbol : :class:`pybamm.Symbol`
   418	            Symbol with Parameter instances replaced by Value
   419	
   420	        """
   421	
   422	        try:
   423	            return self._processed_symbols[symbol.id]
   424	        except KeyError:
   425	            processed_symbol = self._process_symbol(symbol)
   426	
   427	            self._processed_symbols[symbol.id] = processed_symbol
   428	            return processed_symbol
   429	
   430	    def _process_symbol(self, symbol):
   431	        """ See :meth:`ParameterValues.process_symbol()`. """
   432	
   433	        if isinstance(symbol, pybamm.Parameter):
   434	            value = self[symbol.name]
   435	            if isinstance(value, numbers.Number):
   436	                # Scalar inherits name (for updating parameters) and domain (for
   437	                # Broadcast)
   438	                return pybamm.Scalar(value, name=symbol.name, domain=symbol.domain)
   439	            elif isinstance(value, pybamm.InputParameter):
   440	                value.domain = symbol.domain
   441	                return value
   442	
   443	        elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.FunctionParameter):
   444	            new_children = [self.process_symbol(child) for child in symbol.children]
   445	            function_name = self[symbol.name]
   446	
   447	            # Create Function or Interpolant or Scalar object
   448	            if isinstance(function_name, tuple):
   449	                # If function_name is a tuple then it should be (name, data) and we need
   450	                # to create an Interpolant
   451	                name, data = function_name
   452	                function = pybamm.Interpolant(data, *new_children, name=name)
   453	            elif isinstance(function_name, numbers.Number):
   454	                # If the "function" is provided is actually a scalar, return a Scalar
   455	                # object instead of throwing an error.
   456	                # Also use ones_like so that we get the right shapes
   457	                function = pybamm.Scalar(
   458	                    function_name, name=symbol.name
   459	                ) * pybamm.ones_like(*new_children)
   460	            else:
   461	                # otherwise evaluate the function to create a new PyBaMM object
   462	                function = function_name(*new_children)
   463	            # Differentiate if necessary
   464	            if symbol.diff_variable is None:
   465	                function_out = function
   466	            else:
   467	                # return differentiated function
   468	                new_diff_variable = self.process_symbol(symbol.diff_variable)
   469	                function_out = function.diff(new_diff_variable)
   470	            # Process again just to be sure
   471	            return self.process_symbol(function_out)
   472	
   473	        elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.BinaryOperator):
   474	            # process children
   475	            new_left = self.process_symbol(symbol.left)
   476	            new_right = self.process_symbol(symbol.right)
   477	            # make new symbol, ensure domain remains the same
   478	            new_symbol = symbol._binary_new_copy(new_left, new_right)
   479	            new_symbol.domain = symbol.domain
   480	            return new_symbol
   481	
   482	        # Unary operators
   483	        elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.UnaryOperator):
   484	            new_child = self.process_symbol(symbol.child)
   485	            new_symbol = symbol._unary_new_copy(new_child)
   486	            # ensure domain remains the same
   487	            new_symbol.domain = symbol.domain
   488	            return new_symbol
   489	
   490	        # Functions
   491	        elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.Function):
   492	            new_children = [self.process_symbol(child) for child in symbol.children]
   493	            return symbol._function_new_copy(new_children)
   494	
   495	        # Concatenations
   496	        elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.Concatenation):
   497	            new_children = [self.process_symbol(child) for child in symbol.children]
   498	            return symbol._concatenation_new_copy(new_children)
   499	
   500	        else:

     8	class InputParameter(pybamm.Symbol):
     9	    """A node in the expression tree representing an input parameter
    10	
    11	    This node's value can be set at the point of solving, allowing parameter estimation
    12	    and control
    13	
    14	    Parameters
    15	    ----------
    16	    name : str
    17	        name of the node
    18	
    19	    """
    20	
    21	    def __init__(self, name):
    22	        super().__init__(name)
    23	
    24	    def new_copy(self):
    25	        """ See :meth:`pybamm.Symbol.new_copy()`. """
    26	        return InputParameter(self.name)
    27	
    28	    def _evaluate_for_shape(self):
    29	        """
    30	        Returns the scalar 'NaN' to represent the shape of a parameter.
    31	        See :meth:`pybamm.Symbol.evaluate_for_shape()`
    32	        """
    33	        return np.nan
    34	
    35	    def _jac(self, variable):
    36	        """ See :meth:`pybamm.Symbol._jac()`. """
    37	        return pybamm.Scalar(0)
    38	
    39	    def _base_evaluate(self, t=None, y=None, u=None):
    40	        # u should be a dictionary
    41	        # convert 'None' to empty dictionary for more informative error
    42	        if u is None:
    43	            u = {}
    44	        if not isinstance(u, dict):
    45	            # if the special input "shape test" is passed, just return 1
    46	            if u == "shape test":
    47	                return 1
    48	            raise TypeError("inputs u should be a dictionary")
    49	        try:
    50	            return u[self.name]
    51	        # raise more informative error if can't find name in dict
    52	        except KeyError:
    53	            raise KeyError("Input parameter '{}' not found".format(self.name))

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me understand the issue better. Looking at the mesh code at lines 96-100 in meshes.py, when it encounters a symbol, it calls `sym.evaluate()` directly. But if that symbol is an InputParameter, it won't have the inputs available at mesh creation time!

Let me create a test to reproduce the exact issue mentioned in the problem description:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the code more carefully, I think I understand the issue now. The problem is in the mesh generation process.

In meshes.py lines 96-100:
```python
if isinstance(sym, pybamm.Symbol):
    sym_eval = sym.evaluate()
    geometry[domain][prim_sec_tabs][spatial_variable][
        lim
    ] = sym_eval
```

This tries to evaluate symbols in the geometry during mesh creation. However, if the symbol is an InputParameter (like "Electrode height [m]" in our case), it will fail because InputParameters can only be evaluated with proper inputs, and at mesh creation time, those inputs may not be available.

The issue description says "something to do with mesh", and indeed the problem is that mesh generation tries to evaluate InputParameters before the inputs are available.

So the real issue is that when geometry contains InputParameters, the mesh generation fails because it tries to evaluate them too early.

Test script to reproduce and verify fix for the mesh InputParameter issue.

Issue: Solution[<variable>] doesn't work when some parameters are inputs
Root cause: Mesh creation tries to evaluate InputParameters without inputs available

import pybamm
import numpy as np


def test_mesh_with_input_parameters():
    """Test that mesh creation works with InputParameters in geometry."""
    print("Testing mesh creation with InputParameters...")
    
    # Set up model with a geometry-affecting parameter as input
    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    geometry = model.default_geometry
    param = model.default_parameter_values
    
    # Make a geometry parameter an input
    param.update({
        "Negative electrode thickness [m]": "[input]"
    })
    
    # Process model and geometry
    param.process_model(model)
    param.process_geometry(geometry)
    
    # Try to create mesh - this should fail in the original code
    var = pybamm.standard_spatial_vars
    var_pts = {var.x_n: 5, var.x_s: 5, var.x_p: 5, var.r_n: 10, var.r_p: 10}
    submesh_types = model.default_submesh_types
    
    try:
        mesh = pybamm.Mesh(geometry, submesh_types, var_pts)
        print("SUCCESS: Mesh created successfully with InputParameters")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"FAILED: {e}")
        return False


def test_simulation_with_input_parameters():
    """Test that full simulation works with InputParameters."""
    print("Testing full simulation with InputParameters...")
    
    I_typical = 1.0

    def current_function(t):
        return pybamm.InputParameter("Current")

    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    geometry = model.default_geometry
    param = model.default_parameter_values
    
    # Make both current and geometry parameters inputs
    param.update({
        "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
        "Current function [A]": current_function,
        "Current": "[input]",
        "Negative electrode thickness [m]": "[input]"
    })
    
    param.process_model(model)
    param.process_geometry(geometry)
    
    var = pybamm.standard_spatial_vars
    var_pts = {var.x_n: 5, var.x_s: 5, var.x_p: 5, var.r_n: 10, var.r_p: 10}
    spatial_methods = model.default_spatial_methods
    solver = model.default_solver
    
    try:
        sim = pybamm.Simulation(
            model=model,
            geometry=geometry,
            parameter_values=param,
            var_pts=var_pts,
            spatial_methods=spatial_methods,
            solver=solver,
        )
        
        inputs = {
            'Current': I_typical,
            'Negative electrode thickness [m]': 0.0001
        }
        
        sim.solve(t_eval=np.linspace(0, 0.01, 100), inputs=inputs)
        result = sim.solution['Time [h]'](sim.solution.t)
        print("SUCCESS: Full simulation completed successfully")
        print(f"Result shape: {result.shape}")
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"FAILED: {e}")
        return False


if __name__ == "__main__":

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now let me analyze the problem and determine the fix:

**Problem Statement:**
When geometry expressions contain InputParameters, mesh creation fails because the mesh code tries to evaluate those InputParameters without providing the necessary inputs.

**Location of Problem:**
File: `/workspace/pybamm-team__PyBaMM__0.1/pybamm/meshes/meshes.py`
Lines: 96-100

```python
if isinstance(sym, pybamm.Symbol):
    sym_eval = sym.evaluate()  # <-- This fails for InputParameters
    geometry[domain][prim_sec_tabs][spatial_variable][
        lim
    ] = sym_eval
```

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
1. We make "Negative electrode thickness [m]" an InputParameter
2. This parameter is used in geometry expressions to compute normalized positions
3. During mesh creation, the code tries to evaluate these expressions
4. The evaluation fails because InputParameters require inputs that aren't available yet

**Best practices for the fix:**
1. Don't try to evaluate InputParameters during mesh creation - they should remain symbolic
2. Only evaluate constant expressions that can be resolved without inputs
3. Preserve the symbolic nature of InputParameters for later evaluation with proper inputs
4. Handle the case gracefully when evaluation fails due to missing inputs

**How to fix the problem:**
The fix should catch the case where evaluation fails due to missing InputParameters and leave those symbols unevaluated. The mesh creation should work with symbolic expressions, not just numeric ones.

Looking at the code, the evaluation is done to "simplify" the geometry expressions, but it's not strictly necessary. The submesh constructors should be able to handle symbolic expressions.

Actually, looking more carefully, I think the better approach is to only evaluate expressions that can be evaluated without inputs, and leave InputParameters as they are.

    12	    1D submesh class.
    13	    Contains the position of the nodes, the number of mesh points, and

    15

    17	    ----------

    19	        An array containing the points corresponding to the edges of the submesh
    20	    coord_sys : string
    21	        The coordinate system of the submesh

    30	        self.edges = edges
    31	        self.nodes = (self.edges[1:] + self.edges[:-1]) / 2
    32	        self.d_edges = np.diff(self.edges)
    33	        self.d_nodes = np.diff(self.nodes)
    34	        self.npts = self.nodes.size

    36

    43	                return abs(x - point) < tol
    44

    46	                tab_location = tabs[tab]["z_centre"]
    47	                if near(tab_location, 0):
    48	                    self.tabs[tab + " tab"] = "left"
    49	                elif near(tab_location, l_z):

    96	class Exponential1DSubMesh(SubMesh1D):
    97	    """
    98	    A class to generate a submesh on a 1D domain in which the points are clustered
    99	    close to one or both of boundaries using an exponential formula on the interval
   100	    [a,b].
   101	
   102	    If side is "left", the gridpoints are given by
   103	
   104	    .. math::
   105	        x_{k} = (b-a) + \\frac{\\exp{\\alpha k / N} - 1}{\\exp{\\alpha} - 1}} + a,
   106	
   107	    for k = 1, ..., N, where N is the number of nodes.
   108	
   109	    Is side is "right", the gridpoints are given by
   110	
   111	    .. math::
   112	        x_{k} = (b-a) + \\frac{\\exp{-\\alpha k / N} - 1}{\\exp{-\\alpha} - 1}} + a,
   113	
   114	    for k = 1, ..., N.
   115	
   116	    If side is "symmetric", the first half of the interval is meshed using the
   117	    gridpoints
   118	
   119	    .. math::
   120	        x_{k} = (b/2-a) + \\frac{\\exp{\\alpha k / N} - 1}{\\exp{\\alpha} - 1}} + a,

    """Test that mesh creation works with InputParameters in geometry."""

import pybamm
import numpy as np


def test_original_issue():
    """Test the exact code from the issue description."""
    print("Testing original issue code...")
    
    pybamm.set_logging_level('WARNING')  # Reduce noise

    I_typical = 1.0

    def current_function(t):
        return pybamm.InputParameter("Current")

    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    geometry = model.default_geometry
    param = model.default_parameter_values
    param.update(
        {
            "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
            "Current function [A]": current_function,
            "Current": "[input]",
            "Electrode height [m]": "[input]",
        }
    )
    param.process_model(model)
    param.process_geometry(geometry)
    var = pybamm.standard_spatial_vars
    var_pts = {var.x_n: 5, var.x_s: 5, var.x_p: 5, var.r_n: 10, var.r_p: 10}
    spatial_methods = model.default_spatial_methods

    solver = model.default_solver
    sim = pybamm.Simulation(
        model=model,
        geometry=geometry,
        parameter_values=param,
        var_pts=var_pts,
        spatial_methods=spatial_methods,
        solver=solver,
    )

    inputs = {
            'Current': I_typical,
            'Electrode height [m]': 0.1
            }

    try:
        sim.solve(t_eval=np.linspace(0, 0.01, 100), inputs=inputs)
        result = sim.solution['Time [h]'](sim.solution.t)
        print(f"SUCCESS: Original issue code works! Result shape: {result.shape}")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"FAILED: {e}")
        return False


def test_single_vs_double_input():
    """Compare single input vs double input behavior."""
    print("\nComparing single vs double input...")
    
    pybamm.set_logging_level('WARNING')
    I_typical = 1.0

    def current_function(t):
        return pybamm.InputParameter("Current")

    # Test 1: Single input (Current only)
    print("  Testing single input (Current only)...")
    model1 = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    geometry1 = model1.default_geometry
    param1 = model1.default_parameter_values
    param1.update({
        "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
        "Current function [A]": current_function,
        "Current": "[input]",
    })
    param1.process_model(model1)
    param1.process_geometry(geometry1)
    var = pybamm.standard_spatial_vars
    var_pts = {var.x_n: 5, var.x_s: 5, var.x_p: 5, var.r_n: 10, var.r_p: 10}
    spatial_methods1 = model1.default_spatial_methods
    solver1 = model1.default_solver
    sim1 = pybamm.Simulation(
        model=model1,
        geometry=geometry1,
        parameter_values=param1,
        var_pts=var_pts,
        spatial_methods=spatial_methods1,
        solver=solver1,
    )
    
    try:
        sim1.solve(t_eval=np.linspace(0, 0.01, 100), inputs={'Current': I_typical})
        result1 = sim1.solution['Terminal voltage [V]'](sim1.solution.t)
        print(f"    Single input SUCCESS! Shape: {result1.shape}")
        single_success = True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"    Single input FAILED: {e}")
        single_success = False

    # Test 2: Double input (Current + Electrode height)
    print("  Testing double input (Current + Electrode height)...")
    model2 = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    geometry2 = model2.default_geometry
    param2 = model2.default_parameter_values
    param2.update({
        "Typical current [A]": I_typical,
        "Current function [A]": current_function,
        "Current": "[input]",
        "Electrode height [m]": "[input]",
    })
    param2.process_model(model2)
    param2.process_geometry(geometry2)
    spatial_methods2 = model2.default_spatial_methods
    solver2 = model2.default_solver
    sim2 = pybamm.Simulation(
        model=model2,
        geometry=geometry2,
        parameter_values=param2,
        var_pts=var_pts,
        spatial_methods=spatial_methods2,
        solver=solver2,
    )
    
    try:
        sim2.solve(t_eval=np.linspace(0, 0.01, 100), inputs={
            'Current': I_typical,
            'Electrode height [m]': 0.1
        })
        result2 = sim2.solution['Terminal voltage [V]'](sim2.solution.t)
        print(f"    Double input SUCCESS! Shape: {result2.shape}")
        double_success = True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"    Double input FAILED: {e}")
        double_success = False

    if single_success and double_success:
        print("  Both single and double input work - issue is FIXED!")
        return True
    else:
        print("  Issue NOT fixed")
        return False
